# GENESIS — every revision shipped to options_trader_v4

**One line per tarball, in order, from r1.**
**Appended as part of the land command (WORKING_AGREEMENT §33), never after.**

This is not `git log`. Git records what changed; this records **why**, and it
keeps the corrections — several revisions below exist because an earlier one was
wrong, and those are the entries worth reading.

---

## Why this repo exists

OTV3 was closed on 2026-08-19 after its central premise was **measured false**,
not abandoned on a hunch. `tests/direction_skill.py`, 715 closed directional
trades over 16 sessions with ORB and neutral structures excluded: the regime
classifier picked the correct **side** on **44.9%**, 95% CI **[41.3%, 48.6%]** —
the interval sits entirely below a coin flip. Puts were **34.2%**. The strategy
most dependent on it lost **$5,872** across 660 trades.

What made money was already regime-independent: `orb_trail_stop` 95% win / 107
trades / **+$37,848**, `theta_bleed` 100% / 107. Operator: *"every day where P&L
was green or very green is nearly entirely based on the ORB trade and the
quality of our stops."*

**v4 keeps the collection, the execution and the exits. It replaces the layer in
between.** Full evidence in `docs/INHERITED_FINDINGS.md`.

---

---

## What the corrections say

Six of these twenty-five exist because an earlier one was wrong, and the pattern
is consistent enough to name: **the measuring instrument failed before the code
did.**

· r3 — a manifest built from a description list, missing 20 files silently
· r4 — an orphan report that flagged 12 modules, of which **only 2 were real**;
  three were live code the resolver could not see, and one had traded four
  times the day before
· r10 — a measure that contained its own predictor
· r16 — a grouping key carrying a per-trade value, hiding 107 trades as nothing
· r19 — a proxy that was never labelled as unavailable, so it became
  indistinguishable from data for the life of a project
· r24 — a threshold set before the measurement that contradicted it

**Every one of them printed something plausible.** That is why WA §33 fails the
land command on drift rather than warning, and why an absent measurement is
reported as absent rather than as zero.

appends with `>>`, so the append point must BE the end of the document. When the
prose sat below the table, r26 and r27 landed underneath it and the two sections
interleaved. Nothing after this table.

| **r7** | `tests/entry_profile.py` — what made a good entry good, per strategy, labelled on **directional correctness** rather than P&L, because P&L conflates entry quality with exit management. |

| **r9** | `tests/opening_bias.py` — does the session opening expose direction, asked of the tape rather than the trade book (797 session-symbol observations, none chosen by a broken engine). |
| **r10** | **Forward-only measurement.** r9's inclusive numbers were partly tautological: the opening candle's own range sits inside "the day's excursion", which is why they rose monotonically with window length (5m 67% → 30m 82%). Measured from the window close instead: **every cell 49–53%, a coin.** |

| **r12** | `tests/tape_harness.py` — what precedes a payable move, mined across ~300,000 bars with a **date-split holdout**, against a bar the chain sets rather than one chosen. |
| **r13** | `tests/magnitude_estimator.py` — can the underlying **reach** the target. Tests ADX beside ATR rather than assuming. |
| **r14** | Strike reachability by **ATR** band. **ADX measured FLAT** — every band from 0-15 to 40-100 produced the same median excursion, so ADX 45 reaches no further than ADX 12. ATR is monotone across the same bars, a 5.6× spread. The ADX table is kept in the output, labelled flat, so nobody re-proposes it. |
| **r15** | `tests/exit_record.py` — net dollars and the **loss tail** per exit reason, not just win rate. |
| **r16** | Normalise the exit reason before grouping. v3 writes the trade's own P&L into the reason string, so every distinct percentage became its own reason — **ORB's entire 107-trade record was invisible under an n<5 floor and printed as nothing.** |
| **r17** | `RunawayContinuation` — the first v4 entry rule. No regime label. ATR gates the trade and picks the strike; **ADX is unused and that is a measurement, not an omission.** |
| **r18** | Expose `pin_concentration` on `GEXSnapshot` — it was computed, used to decide a boolean, and discarded as a local. Plus the test of whether it predicts the pin holding. |
| **r19** | Fetch real **open interest** over REST. `open_interest` was a declared field with **no producer** — 0 on every contract — so `gex_data` fell through to `oi_proxy = 1000 × gamma` and multiplied by gamma again. **GEX has been a gamma-squared surface, not dealer positioning, since it shipped.** |
| **r20** | `GEXPinButterfly` — complete and **PARKED**. Every gate works; it is off because its input is not yet trustworthy. Parked ~2 weeks for real GEX to accumulate. |
| **r21** | `SweepCreditSpread` — sell the boundary a swept pool just became. High sweep → ceiling → call credit; low sweep → floor → put credit. Reads no age-decay, no damper chain, no regime label. |
| **r22** | Its exits: **15% stop and 15:45 hold, no trail, no TP.** It had been falling through to `DIRECTIONAL` in `structure.py` and would have been laddered out at 15:40 with the debit positions, giving away the theta it was opened to collect. |
| **r23** | `tests/sweep_discriminator.py` — which swept pools **held**, from the tape (2,169 events) rather than 34 trades chosen by a gate that could not fire. |

Also corrected a pre-measurement floor that had excluded the best-measuring bucket. |

| **r43** | stress_entry_path: hostile inputs against every entry path, and the 18 bugs it found. Same in the sweep ceiling. Plus NaN/-inf prices firing signals, math.floor(nan) raising inside pierced_strike, and a NaN mark recorded as the MFE peak. |

| **r46** | AUDIT FIXES. F0: my r38 inserted _track_excursion at column 0 above `def evaluate`, which sat INSIDE the class - Python read the class as ending there and the ~2,000 lines below became nested locals of a telemetry helper. ExitEngine had ONE attribute and no evaluate. EVERY intraday exit was dead code for seven revisions behind a green board, because none of my six checkers executes an exit. F2: TC.6 was live on default=1 while I had written NOT DISPATCHED into its own doctrine block without grepping for the dispatch. Plus 8 more, all verified. |
| **r47** | FEED.2 ported to v4 - the body below the header was BYTE-IDENTICAL across all three repos, so one defect, one fix, three places. symbol_map keyed on (dx_symbol, interval) while FEED.2 subscribes the same symbol+interval TWICE, so the RTH 1h route was destroyed at construction and every 1h bar landed under *_EXT. Six days fleet-wide, nothing raised. Also rewrote C5 in the ported test: it grepped for a variable name the code does not use, so re-planting the exact defect left it reporting ALL PASS. |
| **r48** | RTH guard ported from the SMC thread v3.17. The FEED.2 fix corrected STREAMING routes; the restart backfill then wrote 24-HOUR bars into the plain series anyway - plain 1h carried hours 00-23 on equities from 08-05, ~12/hour overnight vs 38-39 in RTH, while SPX stayed clean because an index has no overnight session. Worse than the hole it replaced: a gap announces itself, a series that changes character mid-stream does not. Bars are SEGREGATED to SYM_EXT, never dropped - EXT_INTERVAL is 1h only so an overnight 5m has no other home and DXFeed history is use-it-or-lose-it. |
| **r50** | chmod +x on all 16 root shell scripts. Git does not preserve the executable bit unless it is set in the index, so a repointed box got Permission denied on configure.sh. Found on the LLY throwaway repoint before it could hit all 15 tomorrow - and the same test proved the sync is clean, all eight checkers pass in a box venv, the env survives a hard reset against a different repo, and the gitignore boundary holds. |
| **r51** | Thread close-out: PORT_STATE rewritten as the current-state document, ROADMAP given numbered next-actions for Friday and beyond, FILE_MAP orientation moved INTO the generator (a hand-edit to a generated file vanishes on the next run), and docs/HANDOFF.md written for the next thread. |
| **r52** | HANDOFF: the archive naming convention, which r51 omitted. otv4_<descriptor>_r<N>.tar.gz, sequence never resets, re-issues get a letter suffix - because a second download of the same filename has nowhere to land and tar xf silently re-extracts the FIRST archive, which cost the operator two rounds of being told wrongly they had not extracted the latest file. And the number is PER-REPO. |
| **r53** | The RTH feed gate is removed - boxes subscribe whenever they are up. It existed so a maintenance wake could not put 29 boxes on the wire at once; the fleet is 15 and a normal session already carried ~20 without strain. MAINTENANCE survives as the only stand-down and is now UNCONDITIONAL: the old block slept only until the next open and seconds_until_rth_open returns 0 during RTH, so a maintenance window declared inside the session was silently defeated. Safe because the v4.2 write guard segregates every non-RTH bar to SYM_EXT for EVERY interval, proven by a new born-red checker that drives the real router. |
| **r54** | configure.sh option 7 called an undefined `confirm` where the repo helper is `ask_yn`. An unbound command inside an `if` returns 127, so the branch read as an honest NO: relaxed entry could never be switched ON, and merely opening the item wrote OT_RELAXED_ENTRY=0. bash -n cannot see it - an unbound name is a runtime event, not syntax. New check drives both answers under a command-not-found trap, and pins every root .sh at 100755 IN THE INDEX - measured that cp preserves the DESTINATION mode, so a tarball can never supply the exec bit and 0644 would commit silently. That is the root of r50. |
| **r54** | configure.sh option 7 had three defects. It called an undefined `confirm` where the helper is `ask_yn` - an unbound command in an `if` returns 127, so the branch read as an honest NO: relaxed entry could never be switched ON and merely opening the item wrote 0. It never called reload_daemon, though set_env edits the UNIT FILE, so systemd would restart from its cached unit and the bot would run the OLD value while the menu reported the new one. And the exec bit: measured that cp preserves the DESTINATION mode, so a tarball can never supply it and 0644 commits silently - the root of r50. New check drives both answers under a command-not-found trap, asserts the reload fired, and pins every root .sh at 100755 in the index. |
| **r55** | main.py deletes `_REGIME_ENGINE` and its startup assert - nothing read the variable, because v4 removed both engines it chose between, so the assert was a gate with no consumer that could only refuse to start. It crash-looped QQQ, which carried OT_REGIME_ENGINE=smc from the SMC fork. A GUARD OUTLIVES THE THING IT GUARDED: deleting a feature without deleting its validation leaves a live veto over a value nothing consumes, and stale env on a repointed box is how it fires. check_versions loses the coupled canary asserting the vars presence. Also carries r54: configure.sh option 7 called an undefined `confirm` and never reloaded the daemon after writing the unit file. |
| **r56** | _is_ext_of polarity was INVERTED: it read tho=true as extended, but tho is trading-hours-ONLY, so that is the RTH echo. 1m/5m/15m/1d register only (sym,tf,False), so every RTH echo missed the route table and was DROPPED - the entire intraday tape, fleet-wide, for the first v4 session, with fetch_quote pricing off the prior close. 1h has both keys so it did not miss, it SWAPPED: plain took extended and _EXT took RTH, both current, both wrong, which is why 1h alone looked healthy. The v3.17 write guard re-segregated the misrouted 1h and partly cancelled the error, so the log said RTH GUARD every session and read as the guard working. test_candle_routing asserted the same inverted table and certified the bug green for six days - a check written from the implementation assumption cannot falsify it. |
| **r57** | Regime purge phase A: the dispatch hard gate tested primary_regime (hardcoded UNKNOWN in v4) so it vetoed every tick - the fleet traded nothing all session. Also removed two dead exits and fixed the unawaited OI coroutine, so GEX gets open interest for the first time. check_no_regime is now tree-wide, born red on 29 gates. |
| **r58** | Regime purge Phase B: zero carries tree-wide; gap prior-dir and sweep score were silent zeros, now honest; vix_band; carries now fail the board |
| **r59** | The condor could never be planned. CONDOR_PF_TIMEFRAME defaulted to "daily" while the fork cache keys on "1d" - rails_for returned None on every box every session, and CONDOR_REQUIRE_FORK reads None as stand-down, so a config mismatch presented as the guardrail policy working. Separately _epoch_triple proposed P2 as the tail running extreme, which the 4.4 confirmation guard was obliged to reject; contained-fork build rate 23/40 -> 28/40. New checker drives the whole path, born red on 8. |
| **r60** | GLOBAL_NO_ENTRY_ET deleted - an unauthorized v3 hardcode that vetoed every afternoon entry at DEBUG; per-structure windows bound the day now |
| **r61** | THE FEED MANIFOLD AND THE DERIVED LAYER. Capture everything the wire offers, give each artifact a home, let consumers subscribe: greeks_series and quote_series at full fidelity (all 13 Greeks fields, all 12 Quote fields incl. bid_size/ask_size), extended candles on all five tenors not just 1h, VIX at tenor parity. chain_marks unchanged as the fast read. Derived layer in its own db: indicator_series (ADX/ATR/EMA/VWAP with accumulators), fork_series WITH REJECTION REASONS, level_ledger with the operator lifecycle, surface_series, fire_snapshot. CHARM and VANNA computable for the first time - both are derivatives of delta over a series and chain_marks last-write-wins made them impossible. Realised vol, VRP and expected move that DECAYS through the session. Condor anchors 1h per operator ruling. Derivers never gate and absence is never zero - both mutation-proven. |
| **r62** | /derived - one deriving engine per derived store. base.py enforces contributor-never-gate structurally: run() wraps derive() and run_all() wraps run(), each mutation-proven independently after an earlier draft passed with either guard removed. indicators (ADX per timeframe, VWAP accumulators, session anchor reset), forks (records REJECTION REASONS and NO_FRAME rather than silence), levels (operator lifecycle - a touch is a hold, a close through retires the level, provenance walk), surface (charm and vanna from the greeks series), snapshot (fire-time payload for the edge study). Registry is the single assembly point. |
| **r63** | The derived layer is WIRED. Engines built once in BotState, driven at the end of assemble_market_state - ONE assembly point, so no derived port can be present for one consumer and absent for another the way ctx chain/gex are. New ctx ports: charm, vanna, levels (nearest each way WITH PROVENANCE and touch score), atm_iv, iv_slope, realised vol cc and parkinson, variance_risk_premium, expected_move_iv and straddle, session_fraction_remaining - every key set even when None. Fire snapshot hooked at both trade-write sites. Caught before shipping: the first snapshot helper reached for a _bot_state global that does not exist, so it would have written zero snapshots silently forever with every check green. |
| **r64** | MANIFOLD PART 2 - the five event types the feed never asked for are subscribed and housed. TimeAndSale gives prints WITH aggressor_side (buy vs sell initiated - real order flow, which FRC.1 was blind to). Underlying gives put_call_ratio and front/back volatility term structure from the vendor. Summary gives prev_day_close_price, a second independent source for the scalar runaway_continuation takes as prev_close and which was absent from the warehouse on 2026-08-21. Plus Trade and TheoPrice. Ten of ten event types now captured. Each subscribes independently and fails open per port; the aux drain is wrapped so no new stream can stall the candle loop. manifold_status reports all seven series homes individually. |
| **r65** | The exorcism: the word is gone from the tree; schema physically dropped and proven to refuse; checker scans for the word itself |
| **r66** | Strategy instrumentation - notes, not gates. One strategy_note row per EVALUATION, fired AND declined, each carrying the derived vector that engine depends on: sweep gets aggression at the rejection (replacing pierce depth as a proxy for whether the level was defended), condor gets VRP and channel-over-EM, butterfly gets charm and pin distance over EM, runaway gets tape aggression. ORB is record-only by construction. Hooked in _safe_strategy AFTER fn() so a note can never influence what it records. Plus analysis/order_flow.py (signed aggression, depth depletion, level-relative threat sign) and the counterfactual flow-exit recorder, which journals when a flow exit WOULD have fired and never acts - bos_exit measured 34pct and -7085 dollars, so this one gets measured before it gets trusted. |
| **r67** | FLEET UNBREAK. Three module-level singleton declarations were missing, all declared global inside a function with no binding: _alert_manager (collateral from the r65 sweep - crash-looped every box on boot, AVGO hit NRestarts=23 showing ACTIVATING/auto-restart, which reads like a slow start and was a crash loop), plus _contract_warned and _snapshot_warned in main.py which fire only on the first capture failure inside the trade-write path. Import succeeds for all three; the NameError exists only at CALL time, which is why every check passed. New check_singletons.py CALLS 27 accessors and AST-sweeps for names that resolve nowhere; mutation-proven. TC.6 default flipped ON - it was a silent encoding nobody chose. |
| **r68** | MANIFOLD HEALTH BOARD. One bulb per stream, rollup when all green: tools/manifold_health.py reports GREEN/STALE/MISSING/IDLE per raw stream, per candle tenor and per derived store. Off-hours staleness renders IDLE not red, because a board that cries wolf every evening gets ignored. Only streams trading depends on drive the rollup; derived ports never do. status.py gains the one-line rollup - the line that would have caught 2026-08-21, when the tape died at 09:30 and the blind latch paged once so the silence afterward meant nothing - and LOSES the Strategy UNKNOWN artifact, which now prints only when there is an active plan. |
| **r69** | PLAN LEDGER - intent as a first-class record. A trade is what happened; a plan is what was intended, and they diverge: a plan can produce no trade at all, or two, and it has a lifecycle recomputation cannot reconstruct. The condor plan lived ONLY in memory, so a restart at LEG1_FILLED left a leg live at the broker with no memory a second was planned. Now persisted with its justification (the derived vector at decision time) and its terminal reason - WIPED_BY_RESTART is its own category so the cost of a mid-session deploy is countable. LEG1_FILLED survives a restart deliberately; everything else is recorded as wiped. status.py reads the ledger for active plans, so the line survives a restart where orb_state.json could not. Sets up mining unfired plans as free backtests on live data, which measures the trigger itself. |
| **r70** | PLAN LEDGER WIRED. The condor records every transition (DECIDED, LEG1_FILLED, COMPLETE/EXPIRED/CANCELLED) with the derived vector that justified it and the price excursion for later scoring; ctx threaded through decide() as an optional arg so bookkeeping can never be a precondition of the trade. ORB confirmations are recorded as plans and EXPIRED with their invalidation reason - the four boxes that confirmed break+retest on 2026-08-21 and were wiped by the 10:39 deploy would now be a countable population. Boot-time wipe recording, except LEG1_FILLED which survives because a leg is live at the broker. Every hook wrapped: a ledger write cannot raise into the state machine. |
| **r71** | WRITE_MAP.md - what every box writes and who writes it. FILE_MAP answers what calls what from the import graph; this answers who OWNS which table, which the import graph cannot. 22 tables across three databases with created-by, written-by (by operation) and read-by, generated by tests/gen_write_map.py and checked in the gate so it cannot drift. Flags orphans (tables with no writer: zero) and tables with no external reader (14) - the latter is the honest picture of a night that built collection faster than consumption. |
| **r72** | OPENING-RANGE RESCUE. If today 09:30 5m bar is missing between 09:36 and the 11:00 ORB cutoff, the feed UNSUBSCRIBES 5m and resubscribes from the open - once per session, date-guarded, self-disarming. On 2026-08-21 the feed ate the open and the range only established at 10:01, costing the whole ORB day; get_orb_range fails SOFT in that case, carrying yesterday range as EXPIRED with no raise and no loud warning, which is why it took an hour to notice. Unsubscribe-first per operator: two live subscriptions on one tenor would race to write the same rows. Note: there is NO REST OHLC endpoint in this SDK - DXLink subscribe_candle with start_time is the only historical path. |
| **r73** | NO SILENT GATES. Every rung that can kill an entry now reports at INFO and into gate_disposition, EDGE-TRIGGERED: a gate speaks when its verdict CHANGES and when it clears, not on every tick - five identical refusals produce one line, and a still-blocked gate re-announces at most every 15 minutes with its held time and tick count. On 2026-08-21 the journal held one event type all day and SPX sat refused for 44 minutes with no record of which rung said no. Wired: sweep (invalidated/age/pierce_depth), butterfly (pin_concentration/no_atm_iv/too_near/too_far), condor (no_fork, now carrying the pitchfork REJECT REASON instead of one message covering six causes - and it was previously level-triggered INFO, printing ~240 identical lines per hour). Reporting can never change a verdict. |

| **r75** | CHARACTER - the tape state, two measured axes, no scoring. Persistence is net travel over total range (cc vs parkinson): a chop day reads 0.26, ranging MEASURED not asserted. The state name is a convenience reading of the axes and the middle band is left NAMELESS rather than forced into a bucket. Hysteresis from the v3 F7 result - hold the incumbent unless a challenger clears the boundary by a margin, NO dwell - produced 3 transitions on a driven session shape, inside the operator 1-3 prior. Character is a STATE WITH DURATION; sweeps and breakouts are EVENTS and keep their own homes, so they coexist. ADX, ATR and the opening gap are recorded PARALLEL and feed NEITHER axis - the opening row honestly reads vol=n/a until the baseline forms rather than borrowing the gap, which keeps "does the gap predict the opening character" answerable. Character and gap land on the fire snapshot, making ORB long-vs-short by gap direction a two-column join. |

GATES - the section that answers 2026-08-21: which rung is refusing each strategy, with the reason, blocks moving between gates, and clears. GEX environment on status.py renders as AMPLIFYING / DAMPENING / PINNING instead of TRENDING / NEUTRAL / PINNING: it is a real measurement of net gamma, not an artifact, but "TRENDING" beside the character line looked like a contradiction when the two describe different things - character is what PRICE does, GEX is what dealer gamma does TO price. PINNING keeps its name because it is the thing itself. Display only; the field the butterfly gate and orb_bias read is unchanged. |
| **r78** | FORK SLOPE - computed and stored since the pitchfork was built, read by NOTHING until now. Normalised by the fork own atr_at_birth so it is comparable across the fleet: the same raw +0.20 per bar is FLAT on a 700 dollar QQQ and BULLISH on a 180 dollar PLTR, and a raw threshold would get one wrong every session. FLAT means FLAT-ISH, a band around zero, because a tenth of an ATR per bar is not a heading. NO FORK PRINTS None WITH ITS REJECT REASON, never FLAT - those are different states. Parallel like ADX and the gap: recorded in ctx and shown on status, feeding no axis and gating nothing; the condor still anchors on the fork EXISTENCE, not its tilt. Read from the stored fork_series row rather than rebuilt, so it survives a restart and costs one indexed lookup instead of a 60-80 bar scan. |

| **r79** | SELF-CLOSE. Each box drains to S3, verifies, and halts ITSELF at 16:45 if nobody else did. The box autonomy is the FALLBACK, not a competing system: with control enabled the conductor closes the fleet at 16:05 and this fires on a stopped machine - nothing happens, and that is the correct outcome, never a warning. It inverts the failure mode, because a disabled or broken control server used to mean fifteen boxes ran all night. Same --verify the conductor uses so the two paths cannot disagree; stays UP if short and alerts for itself; halts the MACHINE (shutdown -h) rather than the services, which is what ends the bill. Persistent=false so a 09:15 wake cannot run a missed close and shut the box down mid-morning. Retires optbot-eod (16:01) and optbot-eod-summary (15:50). |
| **r80** | LOCAL RETENTION POLICY DECLARED, NOT ENFORCED. Per-tenor day counts written into config BEFORE any purge exists to read them, and commented out so nothing acts on them - the numbers can be argued with before a row is at risk. The binding consumer is EMA_ANCHOR=200 and it inverts the intuition: every tenor needs the same BARS, so 1m (390/session) needs 5 days while 1h (7/session) needs 60. Chains/greeks/quotes get 3 days as a RE-PUSH window, verified in source: the surface engine reads a 15-min window and nothing crosses sessions. Lifecycle tables are never purged - a recomputation cannot rebuild a biography. Found while writing it: candle_feed PRUNE_KEEP_ROWS is a FLAT row cap, one number for every tenor, which could not express this policy even if it were switched on. Pruning stays OFF. |
| **r81** | RETENTION PURGE, dry by default, riding self_close AFTER verification and before the halt. That placement is the design: "never delete unverified data" falls out of the ORDERING, since a held box never reaches the line. Daily rather than one-time - a one-shot would have to catch up 40 days in a single pass, the riskiest deletion this system could run; a daily pass takes one day and stays boring. Lifecycle tables (character, plans, gates, notes, snapshots, levels, trades) are excluded IN CODE, not in config, so an edit cannot switch it off - a recomputation cannot rebuild a biography. Verified with planted rows: a 40-day 1m candle goes, 40-day 1h and 1d stay, a 40-day indicator row goes, a 90-DAY CHARACTER ROW SURVIVES. No VACUUM: it would stall the halt for minutes and SQLite reuses freed pages. |
| **r82** | RECONCILE WAS ERASING THE PUSH OFFSETS. `save_ledger(counters, LEDGER_PATH)` wrote the PREFIX COUNTERS over chain_ledger.json, the FILE-OFFSET ledger. chain_ledger maps source path to lines already pushed, so overwriting it means the next push_file finds nothing for its path, resumes at line 0 and RE-PUSHES THE WHOLE FILE - and --verify drains first, so every reconcile/verify cycle re-sent everything and inflated the counter the reconcile had just corrected. Observed live 222 -> 300 while S3 held 74: it got worse the harder we tried to fix it. Nothing lost - content-hashed keys overwrote identical objects. NEW check_ledger_paths.py, mutation-proven, matches the CALL SITE not the constant (asserting both constants exist would have passed against the bug). |

Asks for a bug log with a CLEAN section and a fix tarball per the standing contract. Fable measured it - a trend scored 1.00, a random walk 0.91, and ALTERNATING CHOP ALSO SCORED 1.00 and read as trending. | **r95** | ORB TAPE REACH-BACK, MISSED as a terminal plan state, and per-window manifold freshness. A restart reloaded the ORB RANGE but never its STATE, so a restarted engine returned with no break latches, no attempt count and NO MEMORY OF A RUNAWAY - and a forgotten runaway arms on a break the design exists to refuse, which is a WRONG trade rather than a missed one. rebuild_from_tape replays the session 1m tape through the SAME _advance_state the live loop uses; it needs a session-deep frame because ctx df_1m is capped at 60 bars and at 10:37 cannot see a 09:40 break - the left-edge trap that killed _opening_range until TCS.3. OPERATOR RULING: an interrupted firing sequence is NEVER re-entered. A reconstructed OPEN is recorded MISSED and its attempt CONSUMED; ARMED and a runaway invalidation are KEPT because their trigger is still ahead of us or the point is to refuse. The other half is pinned as hard: a LIVE unfilled confirmation keeps trying every tick, a consumed attempt RE-ARMS so a fresh break still fires, MISSED closes its row and is read by nobody in the entry path, so leg 2 stays permitted. Manifold board: freshness is judged in each stream OWN window, so SYM_EXT and session_summary idle during RTH instead of painting permanent amber - the RTH rollup can reach GREEN for the first time. prints on a cash index renders n/a. SUBSCRIPTIONS UNTOUCHED - we capture everything, always. |
| **r96** | THE ATR UNIT MISMATCH THAT COST EVERY ORB TRADE, plus the stale-dataclass bug in r95's own reach-back. VolatilityState.atr_normalized is atr/price - a FRACTION - and its own comment called it "ATR as percent of price". Every strategy threshold is in PERCENT and traceable to measurements in percent: ORB_ATR_FLOOR_PCT 0.05, RUNAWAY_ATR_FLOOR_PCT 0.08, the ATR-to-delta map, SWEEP_CS_ATR_MAX_PCT 0.20. So the ORB floor demanded a FIVE PERCENT intraday ATR and refused every confirmed break+retest the fleet ever produced. Observed live on NFLX 2026-08-24: textbook confirm at 09:58 ET, chain built, strike priced at C 81.0 mark 0.85 delta 0.389, then "ATR 0.004 percent is below the reachable floor" once per tick for 62 minutes to the 11:00 cutoff - true ATR 0.4 percent, EIGHT TIMES ABOVE the floor. The same mismatch failed OPEN on the sweep, whose 0.20 is a CEILING: fed 0.004 it never tripped, so "too hot for a boundary to hold" has been dead since the split. One defect failing closed in one place and open in another produces no single symptom to chase. FIX: add atr_pct derived from atr_normalized and move the GATES to it; atr_normalized keeps its fraction semantics because five tables already hold fractions and rescaling the producer would change what that column MEANS mid-stream with nothing marking the seam. Fallback is 100x the fraction, never 0.0, because a falsy read would SKIP the feasibility veto on a part-baked box - a veto silently becoming a pass. SECOND FIX, mine: rebuild_from_tape held a local binding to self._data across the replay, but _rearm REPLACES the dataclass, so after the first close-inside re-arm the binding was an orphan. NFLX re-armed twice; the summary printed state=INVALIDATED attempt=1 one line after logging CONFIRMED attempt #3. Not cosmetic - the consume check read the corpse, wrote no MISSED row, and left the LIVE engine CONFIRMED, which before 11:00 would have offered a reconstructed setup as tradeable, exactly what the operator ruling forbids. Every r95 fixture confirmed on attempt #1 and never re-armed, so 16 green checks sat over it; C12 now replays a two-re-arm tape and is born red. |
| **r98** | THE ATR UNIT MISMATCH THAT COST EVERY ORB TRADE, plus three window and ladder corrections. (1) VolatilityState.atr_normalized is atr/price - a FRACTION - and its own comment called it "ATR as percent of price". Every strategy threshold is PERCENT: ORB floor 0.05, runaway floor 0.08, sweep ceiling 0.20. Observed live on NFLX 2026-08-24: textbook confirm at 09:58 ET, chain built, strike priced C 81.0 at 0.85 delta 0.389, then "ATR 0.004 percent is below the reachable floor" once per tick for 62 minutes to the 11:00 cutoff - true ATR 0.4 percent, EIGHT TIMES ABOVE the floor. The same mismatch failed OPEN on the sweep, whose 0.20 is a CEILING, so "too hot for a boundary to hold" has been dead since the split. Adds atr_pct and moves the GATES to it; atr_normalized keeps fraction semantics because five tables already hold fractions and rescaling would change what that column MEANS mid-stream. Fallback is 100x, never 0.0, because a falsy read would SKIP a feasibility veto. (2) MY OWN r95 BUG, found in the NFLX log: rebuild_from_tape held a binding to self._data across the replay but _rearm REPLACES the dataclass, so after the first re-arm it read a corpse - the summary printed state=INVALIDATED attempt=1 one line after logging CONFIRMED attempt #3. Not cosmetic: the consume check missed it, no MISSED row was written, and the live engine was left CONFIRMED, which before 11:00 would have offered a reconstructed setup as tradeable - exactly what the operator ruling forbids. All 16 r95 checks were blind to it because every fixture confirmed on attempt #1; C12 replays a two-re-arm tape and is born red. (3) TCS_START_ET 11:00 -> 11:31. The handoff credit spread and RunawayContinuation fire on the SAME trigger, and AFD.1 blocks long debits at 11:30, so between 11:00 and 11:30 both were armed and dispatch order decided: the runaway wins first refusal, sets signal, and TC.6 sat behind "if signal is None" and never ran. The handoff was shadowed for the whole half hour it was nominally open. 11:31 not 11:30, because the cutoff test is >= so 11:30 would hand both the same minute. (4) Sweep window 15:00 -> 14:00, and relaxed can no longer widen the late side: a vertical opened at 15:00 has 45 minutes to the hard close and cannot collect the theta it exists to collect, while relaxed pushed it to 15:30, which collides with the 15:40 flatten ladder. Early side stays relaxable - opening earlier is a worse example of the same trade; opening later is a trade the session has no time to judge. (5) THE LADDER: execution/entry_ladder.py has been a COMPLETE implementation of the operator ladder since 2026-08-20 with ZERO IMPORTERS, so every live fill has posted a single limit at mark and given away the half-spread - by FRC.1 about 31 dollars per trade against a 2.70 dollar gross edge, an order of magnitude larger than anything on the selection list. Three defects fixed before wiring: a zero BID was treated as an unusable quote, which made the ladder inert on the exact bid 0.00 / ask 1.00 example printed in its own header; the terminal rung snapped to NEAREST so a buy could post 0.48 against mark 0.475 and then clamp to an unpostable 0.475; and the opening rung snapped TOWARD mark, so a dime grid opened at 0.70 instead of 0.80 - conceding 30 percent of the spread before the book had refused anything. All three now round IN OUR FAVOUR and both operator examples reproduce exactly. Plus execution/ladder_registry.py, the missing owner of LadderState between ticks - one rung per tick means the walk IS multi-tick and nothing held the ratchet. NOT YET WIRED into the order paths, deliberately. Plus docs/TRADES.md section 6, the full execution spec, recording three rulings that REVERSE live code: nothing ever crosses the spread so the 15:45 market rung becomes nickel-or-assignment, the 15 percent floor escalates straight to mark rather than walking, and nickel is a 15:45 disposal rule and never a take-profit. |

Four defects of one class - a call never made - survived two code audits because reading cannot find them and calling finds them instantly. The pre-RTH branch now runs assemble_market_state + attempt_new_entry against LIVE inputs (the manifold board at 08:57 and 09:19 shows chain marks, greeks and quotes green at age 22s - the log had no pre-open chain builds because the LOOP NEVER ASKED, not because the data was missing). ENTRY_OPEN_ET gates the two order choke points, entry_engine.enter for debits and _execute_condor_leg for all four credit verticals, so a strategy added later cannot bypass it; EXITS ARE NEVER GATED because a blocked exit is a stuck position. It is a FLOOR not a window - every structures own cutoff still applies. Provenance recorded beside the GLOBAL_NO_ENTRY_ET tombstone so an audit can tell a dated directive from an encoding nobody chose. Plus: a dispatch failure now pages on the FIRST occurrence - the 08-01 blindness rule covered this and nothing implemented it, so fifteen boxes raised on every tick for fourteen minutes and no alert went out. |
| **r102** | THE REHEARSAL STOPPED ONE CALL SHORT OF ANYTHING WORTH REHEARSING. r101 ran the dispatch outside RTH so a never-called path would surface at 07:40 rather than 09:30:01 - but session_guard.can_enter tests the RTH gate FIRST and returns, and attempt_new_entry logs that refusal at DEBUG. Measured on all 15 boxes at 16:53: decided=0 chain=0 - the pass was running and proving nothing, and every skip on the path was invisible. can_enter now takes rehearsal=True, which defers ONLY the two CLOCK gates (outside RTH, past the hard close) - the refusals a rehearsal cannot honour and still test anything - collects them, and reports them in the verdict so a pass reads as "rehearsal", never as permission. Every other gate stays terminal: the 09:35 floor, the butterfly cutoff and the VIX crisis gate refuse a rehearsal exactly as they refuse a trading pass, because those are the answers being tested. The pre-open pass now narrates itself at INFO - SKIPPED / STOPPED at market state / dispatch START / dispatch END - so silence is no longer a valid outcome. TWO CORRECTIONS TO r101, both mine: (1) entries_open() was a SECOND implementation of a rule that already existed - session_guard has enforced the 09:35 floor since v3 and its own comment calls it "the universal floor for EVERY strategy" - so it is now a WRAPPER around is_orb_complete() rather than a rival constant, which is how two gates come to disagree; (2) a bare time-of-day floor is TRUE at 16:53, so entries_open now carries the RTH test too - without it, loosening the RTH short-circuit would have let a rehearsal place. is_rth and is_orb_complete take an optional clock so a check can ask about a specific moment. |
| **r103** | THE ORB STATE FILE WAS WRITE-ONLY AND THE LOOK-BACK WAS ONE-SHOT. orb_state.json has been written every tick since v3 - state, attempt, latches, reason - and read by NOBODY; r95 then built a tape REPLAY to reconstruct exactly what that file already held. Operator, 2026-08-24: "if it HAD the orb state, but was interrupted, it should have written it down. It can easily confirm orb state after any restart, so a 1x look back function is grossly underpowered." The file is now READ at startup and AUTHORITATIVE ("if it wrote it, trust it"), carrying state, attempt count, break latches, break candle, confirmed_at, targets and the last consumed bar; a file from another date is ignored outright. It is written from the engine own snapshot so writer and reader cannot drift. THE ONE-SHOT LATCH IS GONE: _rebuilt_date was set both by a successful replay AND by a replay that found fewer than three session bars, so ONE thin or failed fetch spent the session only attempt and nothing retried - the path that made "a crash loop never recovers" true. The rebuild is owed until the engine knowledge reaches the present, and it is idempotent, so there is no reason to ration it; a resumed engine still owes a gap-fill for the bars written since the file. AND A MISS IS NOW ADJUDICATED BEFORE THE ROW CLOSES: the tape cannot tell a setup this process never saw from one a PREVIOUS process saw, took and recorded before it died, so trade_logger.orb_entry_since asks the trades table whether an ORB entry exists at or after the confirming bar - if it fired, no MISSED row is written. A genuine miss journals as a DISPOSITION with the setup full geometry (range, break candle, retest depth, stop, targets, latches), like a decline, so missed and declined are one query apart. |
| **r104** | THE ENTRY LADDER IS WIRED. execution/entry_ladder.py has been a complete, correct, UNCALLED implementation since 2026-08-20 - it appeared in the orphan report and was dismissed as noise twice - while every live fill posted a single limit at mark and walked away. By FRC.1 that is the largest single item on the board: gross edge +2.70 dollars/trade against 126 dollars/trade of round-trip friction, so capturing half the half-spread is worth about 31 dollars/trade, an order of magnitude more than anything on the trade-selection list. Single-leg and butterfly entries now post ONE RUNG PER TICK through ladder_registry: 25 percent in from the best price, one venue increment toward mark, ratcheted so a refused price never returns, repriced from the CURRENT mark each attempt, and never worse than mark. The walk is keyed to the INTENT (symbol|open|structure) so the next tick RESUMES it rather than restarting at rung 1 - a ladder that restarts every tick re-offers rung 1 forever, which is v3 exact defect. A refusal advances the rung; a fill clears the walk so no stale ratchet outlives its order. The butterfly prices against the STRUCTURE quote built conservatively (ask on what we buy, bid on what we sell) rather than a bare mark. confirm_order_fill gains an optional per-call deadline because a rung needs a SLICE of the entry budget - spending all 20s on rung 1 against a 15s tick means the walk never reaches mark inside a fill window. PAPER IS UNTOUCHED: limit_ladder stays the single paper-pricing authority, because a ladder in paper models fills we have no evidence for. EXITS AND THE ROLL ARE NOT WIRED YET - r105. |
| **r105** | THE EXIT LADDER, THE ROLL WALK, AND ALL FOUR TRIGGERS ON THE SECOND LEG. Per TRADES.md section 6: structural and profit-side closes WALK (25 percent in from best, one venue increment toward mark, ratcheted, never worse than mark); the 15 PERCENT FLOOR goes STRAIGHT TO MARK and re-prices there, because a thesis-invalidated stop that spends six ticks hunting a better fill has turned a floor into a negotiation; the CREDIT hard close takes the nickel or takes ASSIGNMENT and NEVER crosses, reversing hard_close_order_mode for credits only. DEBITS KEEP THE CROSS - section 6 prose and table disagreed and the assignment argument is about SHORT verticals: a long that does not close does not assign, it EXPIRES WORTHLESS, so there is no assignment tradeoff to take and paying the spread beats losing the premium. The exit path had only ever been handed a MARK, so position_manager now stashes the STRUCTURE two-sided quote per tick, built per leg and conservatively (closing a short vertical pays short ask minus long bid at the touch) - limit_ladder v1.1 dropped its synthetic walk because "the shade was guesswork about a spread we cannot see"; now we can see it. THE ROLL OPEN HALF WALKS WITHIN ITS CALL and not across ticks: by the time it runs the old untested vertical is already closed, so check_and_execute_roll sees len(legs)!=2 next tick and never re-enters - an unfilled rung would strand the position half-rolled with the tested side unhedged. Bounded rungs in-line ending at mark, which is the price the pre-r105 code posted immediately. AND THE SECOND-LEG WINDOW TRIES ALL FOUR TRIGGERS: its own header said all four were eligible and the code tried the two forks, so a sweep or TC.6 first leg could never pair and the condor could only ever form from a fork - "a second leg is allowed, never expected" was true by omission rather than by design. Found while wiring it: that block sits in main_loop and referenced macro and ms, which are LOCALS OF attempt_new_entry - a NameError the loop catch-all would have swallowed as an ordinary tick failure. |
| **r106** | THE TENT, AND THE END OF THE PLAN VOCABULARY. Operator, 2026-08-24: "There is no condor implied, it is merely PERMITTED" and "there is no longer a standalone leg - we manage the 1st leg as an individual vertical spread now." THREE THINGS FOLLOWED. (1) THE TAKE-PROFIT IS RETIRED. condor_tp was gated on not _condor_sibling_open, which READ as "only on a standalone" - written when a lone vertical was understood as half of a planned condor waiting for its partner. Under r90 every vertical is standalone, so it was not a rare arm for an unusual case, it was the ORDINARY exit for the ORDINARY trade, and it contradicted the measurement sitting three lines above it: 18 lone verticals, TP@25 percent turned -242.77 dollars into -8.43, and on 28 paired legs a TP was worse AT EVERY LEVEL. (2) ONE STOP NUMBER. The 25 percent was still being written onto every credit row as stop_premium, and quoted in the entry alert, while the live arm evaluated 15 - so the row, the alert and any audit reading either stated a rule the engine no longer applied. CONDOR_STOP_LOSS_PCT is now an alias for CONDOR_LONE_STOP_PCT (0.15), which fixes both forks, the roll and the leg executor without touching six files. (3) condor_leg_num is ALWAYS 0: two verticals that happen to coexist have no first and no second. PLUS THE TENT, the operator post-roll escalation, his rungs 2 and 3 combined at his instruction: on a 1-min CLOSE beyond a short strike of an ALREADY-ROLLED structure, take the PROFITABLE side off - computed from marks, never assumed, so the unresolved question of which short gets breached cannot produce a wrong answer - and buy a long of the OPPOSITE type, equidistant from the remaining short as its existing wing, boxing price in. PRICED BEFORE IT IS PAID: if the hedge debit alone breaches the 15 percent floor on cumulative credit the tent is not built and the whole structure closes. The survivor is RE-BOOKED as Structure.TENT carrying CUMULATIVE credit (original + rolls - hedge debit) so the floor measures the adjusted position; TENT routes FIRST in structure.of and in the fill readback because it keeps the vertical strategy string and every other test would claim it - a misroute hands the hedge long to the directional trail, which is the 2026-08-14 identity bug exactly. Closes as ONE three-leg order: legging out of a hedge re-opens the risk it was built to cap. PROVENANCE: the rungs, the floor and the breach definition are the operator; the OPPOSITE-TYPE hedge was one of two options surfaced by a payoff analysis (a same-type long leaves the far tail uncapped) and the operator chose it. |
| **r107** | THE SWEEP SHORT STRIKE IS THE FIRST STRIKE BEYOND THE SWEEP EXTREME. Operator, 2026-08-24: "It swept. That is legitimately a sweep. Sell the 7635." The old rule required a strike to have been TRADED THROUGH and returned None otherwise, which COLLIDES WITH SECTION 2 OWN SELECTION SPEC: that spec PREFERS a shallow pierce (ceiling 0.25 percent, and a deep pierce means a WEAK level not a strong rejection, 1.28 percent median adverse vs 0.46), while the strike rule requires a DEEP one. SPX 2026-08-24: NY Low 7639.01 swept to 7638.17 and reclaimed - a valid sweep of a named pool by 0.84 points - but the strikes below are 7635 and 7630 and price reached neither, so a fully-qualified setup was declined 608 times in one session. The collision only bites on WIDE-STRIKE symbols, seven of the fifteen boxes, so it presented as "no setups" rather than as a rule - a selection effect that was invisible. THE NEW RULE IS ONE EXPRESSION FOR BOTH CASES: among strikes at or beyond the POOL, take the one NEAREST THE SWEEP EXTREME. On a deep pierce that is the strike price traded through - the old answer, unchanged, verified on four cases - and on a shallow one it is the first strike beyond, which is the ruling. The at-or-beyond-the-pool guard is what keeps it from ever selling a strike INSIDE the pool, a level price never broke. STRIKES COME FROM THE LIVE CHAIN, not STRIKE_INCREMENT: SPX 0DTE is 5-wide near the money and 25-wide in the tails so no single constant is right, and FRC.2 own notes call that class of hardcoded list unverified. A truncated chain DECLINES rather than returning a wild strike: nearest always returns something, and without a bound an extreme past the end of the chain would sell a strike hundreds of points away that nobody described. |
| **r108** | THE PRE-OPEN REHEARSAL GETS A GO-RED TOGGLE (devtools item 68, mirroring the feed-maintenance window at 67). Operator asked the right question three weeks early: is there a danger of forgetting it is on when we come off paper. The flag lives on the BOX at data/REHEARSAL_OFF, is read LIVE on every pass (no restart, survives a bake) and its PRESENCE disables - INVERTED ON PURPOSE, because a flag you must create to ENABLE a safety-adjacent behaviour is one that ends up unset exactly when nobody was paying attention, and the rehearsal exists for the morning nobody was. A fresh, rebuilt or forgotten box rehearses. Reading it FAILS TOWARD RUNNING: rehearsing when you meant not to costs log noise, not rehearsing when you meant to costs finding a broken build at 09:30:01. The menu line goes RED with a flag emoji while it is OFF - the opposite polarity to maintenance, which is red while ACTIVE, because an active maintenance window means no tape while an inactive rehearsal means no early warning. THE ANSWER TO THE OPERATOR QUESTION, PINNED IN CODE: the toggle is NOT what makes this safe. Every order site is behind entries_open(), which requires is_rth() AND is_orb_complete() and reads nothing about paper vs live - so the rehearsal cannot place in either mode whether the toggle is on or off. check_rehearsal_toggle.py G5 enumerates the order sites and asserts each is behind that gate, which is the assertion that fails when someone adds a fourth path - the AFD.1 shape, a gate the NEXT strategy does not have. |

| **r112** | DEBUG LOGGING IS A LIVE FLAG, AND THE STATUS BOARD SAYS SO IN RED. The operator: "the whole panel is in DEBUG right now" and, when asked how: "I do not remember HOW we put them in debug, I just know that they ARE." That is the requirement - the fix must not depend on anyone remembering. config.LOG_LEVEL was a bare literal, so the only way to reach DEBUG was to EDIT A TRACKED FILE, and the hotfix launcher runs git checkout -- config.py before every pull, which reverts it: a setting a bake silently undoes is a setting nobody can rely on. Now data/DEBUG_LOG on the box is applied by main AT IMPORT and ON EVERY TICK, so it OVERRIDES however a box came to be verbose - an edited config, a systemd Environment=, a library that called basicConfig - and REMOVING it genuinely restores LOG_LEVEL rather than hoping nothing else got there first. Proven by driving it against a root logger pre-set to DEBUG: first pass forces INFO, silently. LOG_LEVEL also becomes env-overridable so nothing has to edit a git-tracked file again. status.py gains a bug line, ALL CAPS, RED while DEBUG - the same reasoning as the rehearsal line: the operator reads status constantly and per box, so the state is re-asserted as a side effect of ordinary work rather than as an errand. devtools item 69 asks the fleet, prints per-box levels, flags a fleet that disagrees with itself, and only then offers to change it - no marker, no control-side state, the r110 lesson applied on the first try this time. |
| **r113** | A PROBE FOR THE TWO EMPTY MANIFOLD STREAMS, decisive and read-only. underlying_series and theo_series have been RED on every box since the streams were added. Established 2026-08-24: the subscribe BLOCK RUNS (journalctl -u candle-feed shows subscribed <SYM> Underlying and TheoPrice once per connection, 15/15 boxes, ZERO SUBSCRIBE FAILED), and THREE OF THE FIVE STREAMS IN THAT SAME BLOCK POPULATE - Trade to last_trade, TimeAndSale to prints, Summary to session_summary. So it is not a dead path, not a refused request, and not a broken writer. Two candidates remain and they need different fixes: (a) WRONG SYMBOL SPACE - TheoPrice is computed PER OPTION CONTRACT and candle_feed subscribes all five aux events to the plain ticker while Greeks and Quote use the chain streamer symbols and both flow; a subscription to a symbol with no publisher is ACCEPTED and then silent forever, which is exactly the observation. (b) NOT CARRIED - Trade/TimeAndSale/Summary are exchange prints while Underlying and TheoPrice are dxFeed-COMPUTED analytics, and a plan may carry one group and not the other. The probe subscribes both events to BOTH symbol spaces with Trade and Greeks as positive controls, counts what arrives, prints the symbols that published, and says which case it is. It writes nothing and touches no service. NOTE: it is INCONCLUSIVE outside RTH if the controls are silent - run it during the session. |
| **r114** | THE PROBE CRASHED ON PYTHON 3.14. It used the deprecated asyncio loop accessor for its countdown; the BOXES RUN 3.14, where calling that from inside a running loop RAISES rather than warning, so the probe died before it listened to anything. The sandbox that wrote it runs 3.12, where the same call still works - worth remembering as a CLASS: an API that is deprecated-but-working in the sandbox can be fatal on the fleet, the same shape as the ATR unit mismatch and the two-pathname git check-ignore. Replaced with time.monotonic(), which needs no loop; swept the tree, this was the only use. The r1 land gate then REFUSED THE CORRECT FIX by grepping the whole file and matching its own explanatory comment - the gate now parses the AST and looks at CALLS, because a guard that trips on prose about the bug is worse than no guard at all. |
| **r115** | THE PROBE KILLED ITS OWN SESSION. It called get_chain_fetcher().fetch_chain() for option symbols - a SYNCHRONOUS call that spins up its OWN asyncio loop internally and closes it. The shared get_session() singleton binds its httpx client to whichever loop first used it, so by the time DXLinkStreamer asked for /api-quote-tokens the client was attached to a CLOSED loop and the run died with "Event loop is closed" - a hundred lines of library teardown that named everything except the cause. The giveaway printed BEFORE the banner: "OI: fetch failed for 100 symbol(s): ... is bound to a different event loop", and the option arm read 0 symbols because the fetch itself had already failed. Now the streamer symbols come from chain_marks in the feed store - no network, no second loop, and BY CONSTRUCTION the same list candle_feed subscribes Greeks/Quote to. The store path honours $OT_FEED_DB the way candle_feed resolves it, because a probe that hardcodes the default would read a different store than the feed writes and report an empty arm as fact. Session is created INSIDE the running loop with nothing sync ahead of it. |
| **r116** | THEOPRICE WAS ASKED OF THE WRONG SYMBOL SPACE, AND WOULD HAVE CORRUPTED ITSELF ONCE FIXED. theo_series has been empty on every box since the stream was added 2026-08-22 because candle_feed subscribed TheoPrice to the PLAIN TICKER along with four underlying-level events - and TheoPrice is a PER-CONTRACT analytic, so the subscription was ACCEPTED and then silent forever. Proven on QQQ by tools/probe_aux_streams.py: 12 events on 12 option streamer symbols, 0 on the ticker, with Trade/Greeks/Quote as positive controls. It now rides the SAME chunked per-contract subscribe as Greeks and Quote, wrapped alone so an entitlement gap costs this stream and not the chain marks everything trades on, and it is unsubscribed on expiry rollover like its neighbours. ⚠️ THE SECOND HALF IS THE ONE THAT MATTERED: _on_theo called _base_symbol(), collapsing .QQQ260825C495 to "QQQ", and theo_series is keyed PRIMARY KEY (symbol, ts_epoch) - so once the subscription was fixed, ~250 strikes would have landed on the SAME key each second and overwritten one another. The table would have looked populated while holding one arbitrary contract, which is strictly worse than the empty table it replaced, because an empty table is visibly empty. UNDERLYING IS LEFT ON THE TICKER: that IS its symbol space. It published nothing on either arm, but the after-hours probe saw only ONE Trade event on that arm in 45s, so the negative is weak - retest during RTH before relabelling it uncarried. |
| **r118** | THEOPRICE IS UNSUBSCRIBED, FLEET-WIDE. r116 put it on the per-contract subscription and it landed last night; this morning SPX lost its ENTIRE option-symbol subscription at 13:27 UTC - greeks, quotes, chain marks AND theo all stopped in the same instant (ages 688-698s) while ticker-level events kept arriving. options_chain then returned None every tick ("built with ZERO live marks"), so no strategy could evaluate and SPX could not trade the ORB window. QQQ on identical code stayed healthy. ⚠️ THE PER-CHUNK try/except DID NOT SAVE IT, and that is the lesson: it catches an exception on OUR side and cannot catch a venue dropping a subscription it already ACCEPTED. The asymmetry is WIDTH - SPX is 500 contracts (7 chunks x 3 event types) against QQQ 372 (5 chunks), and MU at 406 showed theo drifting to age=194s while its neighbours sat at 0-5s, a second data point for the same story. REMOVED EVERYWHERE RATHER THAN ON SPX: nothing reads theo_series, it has no consumer and never had one, so it buys nothing today and cost a live session when it went wrong. The writer, the table and r116 full-symbol fix all remain, so restoring it is one line once there is evidence about the limit. |
| **r119** | THE ORB SETUP GEOMETRY IS RECORDED - stop_width_pct and planned_r, NULL by default. Operator, watching TSLA and PLTR both stop out STRUCTURALLY within five minutes of firing: "the closer to the range boundary the impulsive candle (stop location) sits, the lower the risk and higher the R-value. We need to start grading orb on that distance." And: "r-value is the report card of this trading system." stop_width_pct is |entry-stop| / ORB width - how deep inside the range the invalidation sits; planned_r is |target-entry| / |entry-stop| - the R the setup OFFERED at fire time, so realised R can later be compared against what was AVAILABLE rather than against a house average. Both are knowable at the entry stamp and nowhere later. ⚠️ RECORDED, NOT GRADED - operator: "observe first. Obviously." Nothing reads them; check_orb_geometry G4 walks the tree and FAILS if anything ever does, which is the assertion that catches wiring an observation into a decision before the data has spoken. The open question needs the data to answer: do deep-break setups FAIL more often, or merely COST more when they fail - a gate answers the first, a size answers the second, and guessing picks wrong. ⚠️ AND GRADING COULD NOT HAVE WORKED WITHOUT IT: the operator noted ORB survivors are "all structurally A unless we start grading how deep the impulsive candle sits inside the range" - PLTR 51 A-grades, TSLA 30, AVGO 29. The letter carries no information for this strategy, so this ratio is not AN input to the grade, it is the ONLY discriminator ORB has. Verified against real fires: TSLA 09:50 stop 23.1 percent into the range, planned R 3.91; AVGO short planned R 3.29. |
| **r120** | THE ORB TRADE CARRIES WHAT THE TAPE DID AT THE LEVEL. tape_vol_at_level / tape_buy_frac_at_level / tape_prints_at_level from the prints (TimeAndSale) stream, which already carries price, size AND the venue own aggressor_side tag - high volume with price not advancing is ABSORPTION, the direct measure of the operator "it cant hold the level" after TSLA fired three ORB longs at 352.16 and lost all three. Band is 0.25 x ATR not a tick count; window runs from the CONFIRMED BREAK to the fire so it spans the fight rather than the instant; aggressor comes from the venue tag rather than re-derived from price-vs-mid. orb_break_ts and atr_at_signal ride on the signal because without them the measurement silently falls back to "last 15 minutes, price-relative band" and answers a different question. NULL when unmeasurable, never zero. AN ATTEMPT COUNTER WAS BUILT AND REMOVED BEFORE SHIPPING - operator: "what does 2 attempts signify? 3? it could mean opposite things." A counter that cannot discriminate is a column, not an observation. |
| **r121** | RISK_PER_TRADE_USD HAS NEVER BEEN A RISK BUDGET. Operator, reading CVX: "How is 2 contracts at 96 dollars costing me 800???" then "it is not risking the correct amount. I do not account for max loss on every trade!!!" The sizer divided the budget by FULL PREMIUM and set max_loss = total_cost - config own comment admitted it in passing ("Sizing is FULL-PREMIUM based, risk unit = position size"). So the knob controlled POSITION SIZE wearing a risk name. Measured on the mornings own fires: NVDA 1206 dollars deployed, stop 4.52, 302 at risk; TSLA 1280 -> 320; PLTR 1400 -> 350, and it lost 310. A 1050 budget was buying ~300 of exposure - a quarter of intent. The CREDIT path never had this: compute_condor_leg_size divides by (width - credit), the true max loss. compute_size now optionally divides by (premium - stop). ⚠️ OFF BY DEFAULT AND CAPPED, because the stop is SOFT - a premium threshold or 1-min close on a 15s tick, not a resting order - so a gap prints through it and costs the full premium. Sized on risk alone NVDA wanted 10 contracts: 6030 deployed to risk 1510, six times intended risk if the stop fails. DEPLOY_CAP_MULT bounds deployment at a multiple of the budget (default 2x, binding on NVDA at 3 contracts) because risk-sizing has no natural bound - the tighter the stop the larger the position it licenses. A capped size LOGS that it was capped: the operator should see when the stop is tight enough that risk-sizing ran past what he will own. AND THE CARD NOW SHOWS THREE NUMBERS INSTEAD OF ONE - "Total Cost" collapsed them, and for a credit spread it was not a cost at all but margin against premium RECEIVED: Deployed (dollars out, contracts owned, still worth something), Capital at risk (to the stop), Max loss (gap through it). Operator: "dollars out, contracts that have value in." |
| **r122** | THE GRADE NO LONGER SIZES THE TRADE - GRADE_SIZE_MULTIPLIER flattened to a flat 1.0. Operator, 2026-08-25: "right now sizing has to be a 1.0 across all trades... In the future, we may fit based on indicators but we are not there right now." IT WAS SIZING ON A LETTER THAT CARRIES NO INFORMATION: the operator own reading is that ORB survivors are "all structurally A unless we start grading how deep the impulsive candle sits inside the range", and the data agrees - PLTR 51 A-grades, TSLA 30, AVGO 29. Worse, where the letter DID vary it pointed the WRONG WAY: TSLA Grade A 30 trades net -2,237 dollars against Grade B 11 trades net +125, so a 1.5x on A was betting MORE on the band that lost MORE. AND IT SILENTLY BROKE THE RISK BUDGET - 1.5x means an A-grade trade risks 1,575 against a 1,050 setting, which is why NVDA deployed 1,206 dollars this morning. Re-sized on the mornings own fires: NVDA 2 contracts to 1, TSLA 4 to 3, PLTR 4 to 3, AVGO (grade B) unchanged at 6 - every position now lands at or under budget instead of over it. The dict is kept and only its VALUES flattened, so restoring a multiplier after a fit is one line rather than a re-wiring. ⚠️ THE BUTTERFLY VIX HALF-SIZE SURVIVES DELIBERATELY: it is a VOLATILITY rule (VIX 15-20), not a grade rule, so it is not the mechanism being retired. Operator: "the butterfly is a debit, but I think we will need to see a few trades before it earns a 1.0 sizing trait. The current is ok for that one." AVGO has ONE butterfly all-time. |
| **r124** | THE STRATEGY-NOTE COLUMN IS `signalled`, NOT `fired` - AND THE CONDOR PAIRING REFUSAL NOW SPEAKS. Operator, reading item 20: "Those have not fired at all." He was right and the header was telling him otherwise. Traced to ONE LINE: main.py `w.writer.write(name, ctx, fired=signal is not None)` - `fired` means THE STRATEGY RETURNED A SIGNAL OBJECT, nothing about dispatch, the pairing gate, the position check or an order. So NFLX TrendCS2nd|499 is 499 TICKS on which TC.6 produced a signal and was refused downstream every time, and CRM CondorLeg2nd|406|0|406 is a live second-leg plan re-signalling on 406 consecutive ticks with a position already open. The old header read "fired AND declined", which scans as traded-vs-not; the gap between SIGNALLED and TRADED is exactly where every dispatch gate lives, which is the space the operator is trying to see into, and the report collapsed it silently - the repo own named enemy, output that renders cleanly while meaning something other than it appears. Counts are also TICK-INFLATED (a persisted setup re-signals every tick; recorded 2026-08-11 as "liq_map.recent_sweep PERSISTS once set"), so the header now says signalled is a count of ticks and points at trades.db for trades. ⚠️ AND THE SILENT REFUSAL IS FIXED: `_can_open_credit_spread` returned False for BOTH Rule 1 and Rule 3 with no log at all, so CRM 406 refusals left no trace and it took five queries to establish that a PUT was open and the second-leg signal was also a PUT. Phrased as the operator asked - state what is OPEN and what it BLOCKS rather than a rule number: "PCS open - only a CCS can pair with it; another PCS is refused" and "both sides already open (PCS + CCS) - the structure is complete, no third leg". The complete-structure case is tested FIRST because `side in sides` is true there too and would report "PCS open" on a finished condor: a true sentence describing the wrong situation. |
| **r125** | EVERY TIMEZONE-NAIVE CLOCK READ IN THE TREE, FOUND AND FIXED. Started from a QQQ manifold board headed "outside RTH" AT 2:12PM with QQQ/1m at age=1s. Cause: manifold_health._rth_now used datetime.now() - THE BOX CLOCK, AND THE BOXES RUN UTC - so it read 18:12 at 14:12 ET and declared the session over mid-session. Not cosmetic: _in_window INVERTS on that flag, so the _EXT after-hours rows counted as in-window and their by-design staleness painted the rollup AMBER. A clock error presented as a data fault, on status.py, all afternoon. ⚠️ THE SAME BUG WAS IN analysis/volatility_measures.session_fraction_remaining, AND THAT ONE CHANGES SIZING: it returned 0.036 at 14:12 ET against a true ~0.42, and expected move scales with sqrt(remaining), so EM was understated ~3.5x all day on every box. It was VISIBLE IN CVX PAYLOAD AT 11:45 as session_fraction_remaining=0.036 and I read past it as "probably another cutoff". A GREP FOUND 10 SITES; AN AST WALK FOUND 11 - the extra one in macro_data, where a UTC roll would answer "Fed Day: No" on an FOMC morning. Fixed: volatility_measures, options_chain 0DTE expiry pick, tenor_select x2, tenor_publish, macro_data, main reconcile scope, setup_scorer date stamp, exit_engine held-minutes fallback. ONE naked read is LEFT ON PURPOSE and documented - a backup FILENAME in segregate_nonrth_bars, which is backend-facing and UTC by the operator own rule: "Backend facing code should render in UTC. User facing code should render in EST." ⚠️ AND THE SAME BUG LIVED IN SQL. The devtools sensor reports filtered on date(ts,unixepoch,localtime) and localtime IS UTC on the fleet, so everything after 20:00 ET filed under TOMORROW - the operator symptom he had been living with: "Any time I run a report for today after the session ends it fails." Replaced with ET day BOUNDS computed on control against the tz database and applied to RAW EPOCH, so no conversion happens on the box at all. THE FIRST VERSION OF THAT FIX WAS -4 hours, WHICH IS EDT AND SILENTLY WRONG IN NOVEMBER - caught by asserting the span, and the gate now proves 23h in March, 25h in November, 24h otherwise. The land gate is an AST WALK rather than a grep, because a grep only finds spellings someone thought of. |
| **r125b** | MANIFOLD BOARD, three operator rulings. (1) AFTER-HOURS ROWS NEVER PAINT THE ROLLUP IN EITHER DIRECTION - "I do not want manifold health degraded for after-hours sources." The prior rule excluded _EXT rows during RTH only, so OUTSIDE RTH they BECAME the rollup, and a VIX_EXT/1h that has not ticked since yesterday is not a fault at 8pm any more than at 2pm. The rows stay on the board with their real ages; the one-bulb summary now answers only "can this box trade", which no after-hours stream can speak to. (2) UNDERLYING IS OFF THE BOARD - probed 2026-08-24 with Trade/Greeks/Quote as live controls and it published ZERO events on BOTH symbol spaces. (3) THEO IS OFF THE BOARD - proven reachable in r116, then unsubscribed in r118 after it took SPX entire per-contract subscription down; operator: "We tried it, it is not worth the traffic burden with no readers." Both TABLES and both WRITERS remain, so restoring a row is one line. The W6 check is INVERTED from what it asserted before - it used to hold that "did not arrive" must not be laundered into "cannot exist", which was right in principle and left two permanent reds; a permanent red for a stream nobody reads teaches the operator to ignore red, which is the only thing the board is for. |
| **r125** | EVERY TIMEZONE-NAIVE CLOCK READ IN THE TREE, FOUND AND FIXED. Started from a QQQ manifold board headed "outside RTH" AT 2:12PM with QQQ/1m at age=1s. Cause: manifold_health._rth_now used datetime.now() - THE BOX CLOCK, AND THE BOXES RUN UTC - so it read 18:12 at 14:12 ET and declared the session over mid-session. Not cosmetic: _in_window INVERTS on that flag, so the _EXT after-hours rows counted as in-window and their by-design staleness painted the rollup AMBER. It was VISIBLE IN CVX PAYLOAD AT 11:45 as session_fraction_remaining=0.036 and I read past it as "probably another cutoff". Fixed: volatility_measures, options_chain 0DTE expiry pick, tenor_select x2, tenor_publish, macro_data, main reconcile scope, setup_scorer date stamp, exit_engine held-minutes fallback. The devtools sensor reports filtered on date(ts,unixepoch,localtime) and localtime IS UTC on the fleet, so everything after 20:00 ET filed under TOMORROW - the operator symptom he had been living with: "Any time I run a report for today after the session ends it fails." Replaced with ET day BOUNDS computed on control against the tz database and applied to RAW EPOCH, so no conversion happens on the box at all. THE FIRST VERSION OF THAT FIX WAS -4 hours, WHICH IS EDT AND SILENTLY WRONG IN NOVEMBER - caught by asserting the span, and the gate now proves 23h in March, 25h in November, 24h otherwise. | **r129** | THE FIRST DEBIT PLAN — RunawayContinuation, and it solves the debit-R problem every debit trade will need (ORB included). A credit spread risk is DEFINED (width minus credit); a DEBIT stop is a SPOT level, so R needs an estimate of what the contract is worth when spot reaches it. OPERATOR RULING: "the stop distance has to dictate the target... we can use some of our available indicators to inform us if a 1-r target passes the sanity check (and address theta separately). We can add a theta burn layer during fitting later, but calculate this one in dollars at entry." So the STOP IS THE ORB BOUNDARY (structural, not chosen) and THE TARGET MIRRORS THE STOP DISTANCE. ⚠️ THE TRAP THAT MAKES THE NAIVE VERSION USELESS: under a LINEAR delta, prem_at_target = prem + delta*d and prem_at_stop = prem - delta*d, so R = 1.00 EXACTLY, ALWAYS, for every debit on every tape - the gate would be decorative, passing everything at precisely the floor and measuring nothing. THE ASYMMETRY IS GAMMA AND IT IS THE WHOLE EDGE OF A DEBIT: a long option GAINS delta toward the target and LOSES it toward the stop, so the same spot distance is worth more up than down. Second-order: prem +/- delta*d + half*gamma*d*d, gamma positive both ways so it lifts the gain AND cushions the loss. VERIFIED IN THE CHECK: delta-only gives R 1.00, real gamma gives 1.11 on a TSLA-shaped runaway, and D2 FAILS if the convexity term is ever dropped. `gamma_lift` is recorded so the fit can see how much of R came from convexity rather than direction. ⚠️ THETA IS IN DOLLARS AT ENTRY AND NEVER NETTED INTO R - operator instruction, and the number is sobering: $4.17 per contract to the close against a $2.90 debit. Visible at entry instead of discovered at exit; a theta-burn layer is a fitting-phase question, not an entry gate. THE SANITY CHECK he asked for is `travel_atr`: a 1R target that the tape cannot cover in the time left is not 1R, and 1.5 ATR is a STATED PRIOR. Four plans now built of eight; ORB stays exempt by ruling. |

Scoring one leg alone systematically understates the trade - a 0.30 side and a 0.30 side are not a 0.30 condor, and K3 pins it. And short-of-risk-free IS no roll: the roll ENTIRE PURPOSE is making the tested side risk-free and the source refuses to execute one that is not (`if plan is None or not plan.risk_free: return False`), so it is not a smaller version of the trade but a different and worse one. BOTH off-the-table cases - no room AND short of risk-free - now fall through to the tent carrying their reason. So `strategy/criteria.stop_survivable()` is a STRUCTURAL gate, never mode-dependent, checked BEFORE `executable()`: the stop must clear the bid-ask ⟨PRIOR⟩ 2.0x, and an UNMEASURABLE quote REFUSES rather than assuming. V5 pins that it never reads the relaxed flag; V6 pins the ordering. ⚠️ UNIVERSAL BY CONSTRUCTION — it compares two numbers off the same chain, so a $9 stock and a $7,000 index are judged identically. That matters because **`WING_WIDTH = 5.0` IS A FIXED DOLLAR AMOUNT** — one strike increment on SPX and SIX on CVX — which is how a 6-wide spread collecting $0.58 looked normal to the code. 🔴 SECOND HALF: THE SPENT-LEVEL LOCK. Nothing remembered the previous attempt, so the pool re-qualified every time price wandered back to its side. `LiquiditySweep.invalidated` (LIQ.3) answers "has the TAPE accepted through" — a fact about the market; it CANNOT answer "did WE already try this and lose" — a fact about us. Both are needed and only one existed. A losing sweep close now marks (symbol, side, pool) SPENT for the day, hooked into `trade_logger.log_exit` because that is the ONE choke point every close passes through. Keyed rounded to the cent (the pool drifts in the last decimal per tick; an exact-float key would never match itself and the lock would silently never fire). LOSSES ONLY — a level that PAID is not discredited by paying. `pool_price` is now persisted on the trade record; it was set on the signal all along and never stored. ⚠️ SCOPE STATED: the viability gate is wired into the SWEEP only. TrendCreditSpread, IronCondor and DailyFork each name their variables differently and use different stop rules — wiring them properly is a larger change than was safe to make while the loop was live. |
| **r155** | THE LONE-VERTICAL STOP IS A FRACTION OF THE **RISK**, NOT OF THE CREDIT — AND THE OLD RULE WAS INVERTED. Operator, 2026-08-27, after CVX entered the SAME 197.5/192.5 spread SEVENTEEN times in twelve minutes, every one dead inside a minute: *"We need to fix the structure that that loop was exploiting."* 🔴 WHAT IT WAS: `stop_level = entry_prem * 1.15` — fifteen percent OF THE CREDIT. **The less credit collected, the TIGHTER the stop in dollars**, so the structures with the least premium got the least room: $0.58 credit -> **8.7 CENTS** of tolerance against $442 of risk; $3.00 credit -> 45 cents. That is backwards — a thin credit means the short strike is FAR OUT and the position is LESS threatened, and it was handed a stop a single tick clears. Enter, noise, stop, re-arm, repeat, sixteen times. NOW anchored to `width - credit`, what is actually at stake, which does not shrink as the credit shrinks: the same 15% gives the CVX structure **$0.663** instead of $0.087 (7.6x), and room now FALLS as the credit rises rather than rising with it. ⚠️ THIS IS NOT THE R HURDLE AND IS NOT MUTED BY RELAXED — the operator asked directly whether this was circular. R is a RATIO judged once at entry (economics; relaxed mutes it deliberately to collect the population it would refuse). This uses only R`s DENOMINATOR, as a DISTANCE, continuously, to decide when to LEAVE. A relaxed R-0.11 trade is still TAKEN — it simply lives long enough to produce an outcome. **A trade stopped by noise is not an observation**: today`s sixteen CVX rows said nothing about whether selling that level works, because none survived to find out. ⚠️ 15% IS CARRIED OVER, NOT RE-FITTED. It meant "15% of credit" and now means "15% of risk" — a different quantity, so the number is a ⟨PRIOR⟩ until the plan table earns it. ⚠️ A MISSING `spread_width` FALLS BACK TO THE OLD RULE AND WARNS LOUDLY — a stop that cannot be computed must never silently become no stop. ⚠️ AND THE EXISTING TEST WAS PINNING THE OLD ARITHMETIC: `check_condor_stop_suppression` had no `spread_width` in its fixture (so it exercised the fallback) and a breach of 1.30 chosen to clear the old 1.15 floor. Both re-derived — the fixture`s numbers encoded the rule being replaced, the same trap as a double carrying a field name that does not exist. |
| **r156** | R IS A CONSTRUCTION TARGET, NOT A FILTER — THE WING IS SEARCHED. Operator, 2026-08-27: *"strike selection must net r of 1 or better"*, *"make the r-value a requirement outright... and relax something else to loosen the entry"*, *"the integrity of the trade mechanics comes first."* 🔴 WHAT THIS REPLACES: a single wing at `WING_WIDTH = 5.0`, a FIXED DOLLAR AMOUNT — one strike increment on SPX and SIX on CVX — with R checked AFTER the fact and MUTED under relaxed. That is how a 6-wide spread collecting $0.58 (R 0.13) looked normal to the code and was entered SEVENTEEN TIMES in twelve minutes. THE SHORT STRIKE IS STRUCTURAL (it comes from the level and never moves); the WING is the only free variable, and the tradeoff is monotonic — narrower wing, less credit, less risk, HIGHER R — so "the wing that best clears the floor" is computable and "no wing does" is a definite answer. Every listed strike beyond the short is now priced on bid/ask and scored; the best R wins, not the first that passes, because a wider wing sometimes pays enough more credit to beat a narrow one. ⚠️ MEASURED ON THE EXACT CHAIN THAT LOOPED (CVX short put 197.5, bid $0.80): 1.0-wide R 0.250 · 2.0-wide 0.227 · 3.0-wide 0.190 · **5.0-wide (the old fixed wing) 0.131** · 7.5-wide 0.098. **NO WING CLEARS 1.00 — the trade is refused by STRUCTURE**, and the old fixed wing was the second-worst choice on the board. ⚠️ NOT MUTED BY RELAXED, AND THAT IS THE POINT. `R_FLOOR` is read DIRECTLY, never through `r_hurdle()`, which returns None under relaxed — W3 pins it and the mutation (routing through the hurdle) turns it red. Relaxed keeps widening the EVIDENCE dials it always did (sweep_max_age_bars 8->24, sweep_pierce_ceiling 0.25->0.75, level_hold_min 0.75->0.50); it no longer waives the economics. W8 pins those three so relaxed cannot quietly become a no-op. ⚠️ CONSEQUENCE STATED UP FRONT AND ACCEPTED BY THE OPERATOR: **relaxed now produces FEWER trades, not more.** Every setup must find a wing that pays for its own risk. An OTM credit spread reaches R 1.0 only when the credit is at least half the width, so short strikes far from spot will simply not trade. Whether that leaves enough sample is a COUNT, not an argument — the plan table records every refusal with its best-available R, and two sessions will say whether any evidence dial needs loosening further. ⚠️ SCOPE: the sweep only. TrendCreditSpread, IronCondor and DailyFork still use fixed wings and still route R through the muted hurdle. |
